2026-10-15T17:41:18.976Z | ASSISTANT | 42f2da55-13ba-4b8b-a107-39ee20d99034
The baseline wins. On validation (24 Aug – 16 Sep), **neither Logistic Regression nor LightGBM beats the simple 24h baseline.** LightGBM does worse than a coin flip.

| Model | Brier skill (all / calm hours) | ROC-AUC (all / calm) | Average predicted P (actual rate 0.57) |
|---|---|---|---|
| **24h baseline** | **+0.15 / +0.19** | **0.75 / 0.78** | 0.47 |
| Logistic Regression | −0.22 / −0.16 | 0.63 / 0.68 | 0.31 |
| LightGBM | −0.90 / −0.86 | **0.36** / 0.28 | 0.14 |
| sklearn gradient boosting (cross-check) | −0.87 / −0.82 | 0.37 / 0.32 | 0.14 |

The 24h baseline turns the number of alert starts in the last 24 hours into a probability; it learns nothing. The sklearn model, with the same settings as LightGBM, gives the same result, so this isn't a LightGBM quirk. Both models pass the scrambled-labels leakage test, and there are now 24 tests, all passing.

## Why: actual versus predicted, grouped by recent activity
| Starts in last 24h | Validation hours | **Actual** | 24h baseline | Logistic | LightGBM |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 16 | 0.25 | 0.00 | 0.23 | 0.20 |
| 1 | 77 | 0.25 | 0.12 | 0.23 | 0.24 |
| 2 | 54 | 0.15 | 0.22 | 0.26 | 0.22 |
| 3–4 | 74 | 0.62 | 0.37 | 0.30 | 0.17 |
| 5–6 | 113 | 0.62 | 0.49 | 0.31 | 0.10 |
| 7–9 | 112 | 0.63 | 0.62 | 0.35 | **0.08** |
| 10+ | 128 | **0.84** | 0.77 | 0.37 | **0.10** |

**1. Both the alert level and the pattern changed.** Before 24 August, a busy day said little about the next 3 hours: the chance of a new alert only rose from 0.12 to 0.19. During the escalation, it rises from 0.25 to 0.84. Both models learned from the calm period (2023 to August), so they learned the wrong pattern. In numbers, `starts_24h` alone scores ROC-AUC 0.56 in training but 0.75 in validation.

**2. LightGBM learned the pattern backwards and can't go beyond what it saw.**
- Only 69 of the 31,941 training hours had more than 6 starts in the last 24h. Validation has 240 of 574.
- In that thin slice of training data, LightGBM learned "very active means *less* likely". If you change only `starts_24h`, its prediction falls from 0.08 to 0.044 and then stays flat from 6 upwards.
- That's how trees work: the prediction is constant beyond the largest value seen in training. Here that constant is wrong, and it points the wrong way.
- Shuffling `starts_24h` *raises* LightGBM's validation AUC by 0.064, so the feature is actively hurting it.

**3. Logistic Regression gets the direction right but is far too low.** Because the features are log-scaled, its predictions keep rising past the training range (0.23 → 0.37), as I expected. But how steeply it rises, and the level it starts from, were learned from the calm period. It also gives most of the weight to `starts_7d` rather than `starts_24h`, and in the escalation `starts_24h` is the one that matters.

**4. The 24h baseline wins precisely because it learns nothing from the past.** It reacts to current intensity straight away. Under a sudden regime change that's an advantage.

**What this means:** with a sudden regime change, a model trained once on old data is the wrong setup. In real use a forecaster would be retrained as new data arrives. So the fix is about the evaluation protocol, not a fancier model:
- **Walk-forward retraining:** refit every day on all data up to that day. This is realistic and leak-free, because each fit only uses the past.
- **Learn corrections on top of the baseline:** give the logistic model the baseline's log-odds as a feature, so it starts from the 24h rate and adjusts it, for example by hour of day or time since the last all-clear.

**How confident can we be?** Validation is only 574 hours across one escalation. The direction of these results is solid, but the exact numbers will be noisy.

Nothing is committed yet. This change set adds models.py (LightGBM, Logistic Regression and the sklearn model), renames `run_baselines.py` to [scripts/run_eval.py](scripts/run_eval.py), pins `lightgbm==4.7.0`, and adds these findings to [DECISIONS.md](DECISIONS.md).

2026-10-15T17:42:19.456Z | USER | c1507d06-7ff2-43b5-a627-102d482057da
[TOOL RESULT: toolu_01D8DUxTN7CU5LXd5S4bAHf5]
14	from .features import FEATURE_COLUMNS
15	
16	LOG_COLUMNS = [
17	    "starts_3h", "starts_6h", "starts_24h", "starts_7d",
18	    "active_min_24h", "active_min_7d", "current_alert_min",
19	    "min_since_last_start", "min_since_last_end",
20	]
21	# "Never happened" (NaN) is treated as a very long time ago: ~70 days.
22	NEVER_MIN = 1e5
23	
24	
25	def logistic_design(df: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
26	    """log1p of counts and durations, so that the log-odds respond to *relative* change in
27	    alert intensity, and that response keeps going beyond the range seen in training."""
28	    x = pd.DataFrame(index=df.index)
29	    for c in LOG_COLUMNS:
30	        x[f"log_{c}"] = np.log1p(df[c].fillna(NEVER_MIN))
31	    x["active_now"] = df["active_now"]
32	    x["hour_sin"] = df["hour_sin"]
33	    x["hour_cos"] = df["hour_cos"]
34	    return x
35	
36	
37	class LogisticRate:
38	    def fit(self, train: pd.DataFrame):
39	        # The scaler is part of the pipeline, so its mean/std come from training rows only.
40	        self.pipe_ = make_pipeline(StandardScaler(), LogisticRegression(C=1.0, max_iter=2000))
41	        self.pipe_.fit(logistic_design(train), train["y"])
42	        return self
43	
44	    def predict(self, df: pd.DataFrame) -> np.ndarray:
45	        return self.pipe_.predict_proba(logistic_design(df))[:, 1]
46	
47	    def coefficients(self) -> pd.Series:
48	        lr = self.pipe_[-1]
49	        names = self.pipe_[0].get_feature_names_out()
50	        return pd.Series(lr.coef_[0], index=names).sort_values(key=abs, ascending=False)
51	
52	
53	class LightGBM:

2026-10-15T17:42:53.473Z | ASSISTANT | f161d11c-a3d9-4e46-af14-2a4adf7f3e6b
[TOOL CALL: Write; id=toolu_01XbSWeKdtr31cFYedxMGgw8]
{
  "file_path": "/Users/oleksandr/code/KSE/AI-Agentic-School/KSE-AI-Agentic-School-Stage2/src/air_alerts/walkforward.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Walk-forward evaluation: refit once per Kyiv day, as a deployed forecaster would.\n\nThe label of a row issued at t is known only at t + horizon. So before day D starts, the model\nmay train on rows with t + horizon <= D, and it then forecasts every hour of D. Earlier days of\nthe evaluated period become training data for later ones; nothing from D or after is used.\n\"\"\"\nfrom collections.abc import Callable\n\nimport numpy as np\nimport pandas as pd\n\nfrom . import config\n\n\ndef known_by(df: pd.DataFrame, moment: pd.Timestamp, horizon: pd.Timedelta = config.HORIZON) -> pd.DataFrame:\n    \"\"\"Rows whose label has been observed by `moment`.\"\"\"\n    return df[df.index + horizon <= moment]\n\n\ndef walk_forward_predict(\n    df: pd.DataFrame,\n    factory: Callable,\n    start: pd.Timestamp,\n    end: pd.Timestamp,\n    horizon: pd.Timedelta = config.HORIZON,\n) -> pd.Series:\n    \"\"\"Forecasts for rows with start <= t < end, refitting at every Kyiv midnight.\"\"\"\n    preds = pd.Series(np.nan, index=df.index)\n    day = start.tz_convert(config.TZ).normalize()\n    while day < end:\n        next_day = day + pd.DateOffset(days=1)  # calendar day, so DST days stay aligned\n        target = df[(df.index >= max(day, start)) & (df.index < min(next_day, end))]\n        if len(target):\n            model = factory().fit(known_by(df, day, horizon))\n            preds.loc[target.index] = model.predict(target)\n        day = next_day\n    return preds[(df.index >= start) & (df.index < end)]\n"
}

1	"""Score baselines and models on validation (default) or, once, on test.
2	
3	    python scripts/run_eval.py            # fit on train, score on val
4	    python scripts/run_eval.py --test     # fit on train+val, score on test
5	
6	The test period is meant to be looked at once, after all choices are made on validation.
7	"""
8	import argparse
9	import sys
10	from pathlib import Path
11	
12	import pandas as pd
13	
14	sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1] / "src"))
15	
16	from air_alerts import config  # noqa: E402
17	from air_alerts.baselines import BASELINES  # noqa: E402
18	from air_alerts.dataset import build_dataset  # noqa: E402
19	from air_alerts.evaluate import evaluate  # noqa: E402
20	from air_alerts.models import MODELS  # noqa: E402
21	
22	
23	def main() -> None:
24	    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()
25	    parser.add_argument("--test", action="store_true", help="evaluate on the held-out test period")
26	    args = parser.parse_args()
27	
28	    df = build_dataset()
29	    if args.test:
30	        fit_on, target = df[df["split"].isin(["train", "val"])], df[df["split"] == "test"]
31	    else:
32	        fit_on, target = df[df["split"] == "train"], df[df["split"] == "val"]
33	
34	    preds = {name: cls().fit(fit_on).predict(target) for name, cls in {**BASELINES, **MODELS}.items()}
35	    results = evaluate(target, preds)
36	
37	    period = "test" if args.test else "val"
38	    def span(part: pd.DataFrame) -> str:
39	        local = part.index.tz_convert(config.TZ)
40	        return f"{local.min():%Y-%m-%d %H:%M} .. {local.max():%Y-%m-%d %H:%M} Kyiv ({len(part)} rows)"
41	
42	    print(f"Forecast issue times. Fit on {span(fit_on)}; scored on {period} {span(target)}; "
43	          f"horizon {config.HORIZON / pd.Timedelta(hours=1):.0f}h")
44	    with pd.option_context("display.width", 160, "display.float_format", "{:.3f}".format):
45	        for name, part in results.groupby("slice", sort=False):
46	            print(f"\n[{name} hours]")
47	            print(part.drop(columns="slice").set_index("model").to_string())
48	
49	    out = config.ROOT / "reports" / f"eval_{period}.csv"
50	    out.parent.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
51	    results.to_csv(out, index=False)
52	    print(f"\nsaved {out.relative_to(config.ROOT)}")
53

56	    main()

2026-10-15T17:44:46.270Z | ASSISTANT | e073597b-e211-4797-86aa-0d4cca614d59
Committed as `9f56924` first, as you asked. Walk-forward retraining is now implemented and leak-tested. It **helps a lot, but neither model beats the 24h baseline yet.** The baseline log-odds feature changes almost nothing, and for LightGBM nothing at all.

## Results on validation (574 hours, 24 Aug – 16 Sep)
| Model | Brier skill, all hours | Brier skill, calm hours | ROC-AUC | Average predicted P (actual 0.57) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **24h baseline** | **+0.15** | **+0.19** | **0.75** | 0.47 |
| Logistic: trained once → walk-forward | −0.22 → −0.12 | −0.16 → −0.06 | 0.63 → 0.62 | 0.31 → 0.37 |
| Logistic + baseline feature: trained once → walk-forward | −0.21 → **−0.10** | −0.15 → −0.04 | 0.63 → 0.63 | 0.32 → 0.37 |
| LightGBM: trained once → walk-forward | −0.90 → **−0.07** | −0.86 → **+0.01** | 0.36 → 0.61 | 0.14 → 0.48 |
| LightGBM + baseline feature | *identical to LightGBM* | | | |

## What works better, and why

**1. Walk-forward rescues LightGBM.** Retrained each day, it sees escalation hours and stops predicting 0.08 where the actual rate is 0.6–0.8. Its skill rises from −0.90 to −0.07, and in calm hours it roughly ties a constant forecast of the period's own average rate (+0.01). Logistic Regression gains less, because the trained-once version already extrapolated in the right direction.

**2. But the new data is outvoted by the old.** At the start of each 8-day block of the validation period, escalation hours are only **0%, 0.6% and 1.2%** of the training set. Every hour counts equally, so three years of calm data outweigh three weeks of escalation. The models drift toward the new pattern far too slowly. That is the remaining gap to the baseline.

**3. Why the baseline feature did nothing for LightGBM.** The predictions are identical down to the last digit (max difference 0.0). Not a bug: the feature is just a rescaling of `starts_24h` that keeps the order of the values the same, and trees only use the order of a feature's values. So LightGBM makes exactly the same splits. For Logistic Regression it adds only +0.01–0.02, because `log_starts_24h` already carries nearly the same information. Your idea was reasonable; this data shows why it can't help here.

**4. The baseline isn't steady either.** Its skill over the three 8-day blocks is +0.36, then +0.13, then −0.05. Each block is only about 190 hours, so treat every number here as noisy, especially differences of a few hundredths.

## The 2 walk-forward leakage tests
- Training rows for day D must have labels known by midnight: t + 3h ≤ D.
- Flipping every label not yet known by D must leave D's forecasts unchanged.

I planted the classic walk-forward leak in a scratch copy: training on rows *issued* before midnight rather than rows whose labels are *known* by midnight. That failed 3 tests: the history test and the flip test for both models. All 29 tests pass on the real code.

## My suggestion for the next step
The remaining problem is how the training data is weighted, not which model we use. So the next step is to **give recent hours more weight**: time-decay sample weights, e.g. a half-life of about 2 weeks. Both models accept per-row weights.

The risk is that choosing the half-life on validation is tuning on validation. I'd fix it in advance at about 14 days and try at most one or two values.

The clock matters too: the deadline is tomorrow 23:59. After that experiment, we should freeze the choice and run the test period once.

None of the walk-forward work is committed yet. The changes are [walkforward.py](src/air_alerts/walkforward.py), the baseline feature in [models.py](src/air_alerts/models.py), the walk-forward tests, and the updated [DECISIONS.md](DECISIONS.md).

2026-10-15T17:48:06.610Z | ASSISTANT | 9fd20393-9420-4942-8dd6-b1426eb5f534
Committed the walk-forward work as `e7a18b5`, then added the time-decay weights. **The 14-day decay makes Logistic Regression the first learned model with positive skill, but the 24h baseline still wins on every metric once one artifact is removed.**

## Results on validation (walk-forward, 574 hours)
| Model | Brier skill (all / calm) | Log loss | ROC-AUC (all / calm) | Average P (actual 0.57) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **24h baseline** | **+0.15 / +0.19** | 0.685 → **0.611** with a 0.05 floor | **0.75 / 0.78** | 0.47 |
| Logistic, no decay | −0.12 / −0.06 | 0.744 | 0.62 / 0.68 | 0.37 |
| **Logistic, 14-day decay** | **+0.06 / +0.10** | 0.656 | 0.67 / 0.70 | 0.55 |
| Logistic, 60-day decay | +0.02 / +0.07 | 0.673 | 0.63 / 0.68 | 0.50 |
| LightGBM, no decay | −0.07 / +0.01 | 0.740 | 0.61 / 0.65 | 0.48 |
| LightGBM, 14-day decay | −0.14 / −0.08 | 0.851 | 0.64 / 0.66 | 0.54 |
| LightGBM, 60-day decay | −0.07 / −0.01 | 0.772 | 0.64 / 0.68 | 0.54 |

## What it shows

**1. Decay works for Logistic Regression.** Brier skill goes from −0.12 to +0.06. Its average prediction finally matches reality (0.55 vs 0.57) instead of being dragged down by the calm years. Both half-lives help, 14 days more than 60, so the improvement doesn't depend on picking a lucky value.

**2. Decay hurts LightGBM at 14 days.** A 14-day half-life leaves roughly **480 hours of effective data**. That's plenty for Logistic Regression's 13 coefficients, but far too little for 300 trees with 15 leaves each, which then fit noise: log loss jumps to 0.85. The rule of thumb: the more a model can fit, the more data it needs. Under a regime shift, recent data is always scarce.

**3. A result I almost reported wrongly.** At first, Logistic with 14-day decay seemed to beat the baseline on log loss (0.656 vs 0.685). I checked, and it doesn't:
- The baseline predicts **exactly 0** in the 16 hours with no alert in the last 24h. 4 of those hours were followed by an alert.
- Log loss punishes a confident 0 very hard, so those 4 hours alone add 0.096 to the baseline's score.
- On the other 558 hours the baseline scores **0.606, against 0.659** for Logistic.
- With probabilities floored at 0.05, the baseline scores 0.611 overall.

This flaw comes from the Poisson formula, not from Logistic being better. It's a good example of checking a "win" before claiming it.

**4. The baseline wins in all three 8-day blocks** (+0.36 / +0.13 / −0.05 against +0.18 / +0.07 / −0.08). With about 190 hours per block, the ranking is consistent even though the exact numbers are noisy.

## Conclusion so far
Under a sudden regime change with only 3 weeks of new data, **"count alerts in the last 24 hours" beats every learned model we've tried.** That's an honest finding, not a failure. The learned models carry three years of calm-period patterns that no longer apply, and the new period is too short for them to learn the new ones.

## Proposal for the final test run (once, frozen now)
- **24h baseline**, plus the same baseline with probabilities floored at 0.05. To be transparent: I'm proposing the floor after seeing the validation zeros. But it fixes a structural flaw, since a probability of exactly 0 is never justified, rather than tuning a number.
- **Logistic Regression, walk-forward, 14-day decay**: the best learned model.
- For reference: the trained-once Logistic and LightGBM, and walk-forward LightGBM. These show the path from the first attempt to the final one, which is exactly what your reflection needs.

Not committed yet: the decay weights, 4 new tests (33 total, all passing), and the updated [DECISIONS.md](DECISIONS.md).

2026-10-15T17:55:04.147Z | ASSISTANT | 99b81e96-da07-4ab9-9487-2b92ba57f72e
The test period has been scored once, as planned. **The test result went the opposite way from validation:** the baseline I picked as primary lost, and the best learned model won clearly.

## Test results (17 Sep – 14 Oct, 670 hours, actual rate 0.70)
| Model | Brier skill (all / calm) | Log loss | ROC-AUC | Average P |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 24h baseline, floored (**chosen as primary**) | −0.105 / −0.250 | 0.658 | 0.54 | 0.59 |
| Logistic, trained once | −0.220 / −0.446 | 0.705 | 0.61 | 0.47 |
| LightGBM, trained once | −0.058 / −0.104 | 0.639 | 0.61 | 0.62 |
| LightGBM, walk-forward | −0.006 / −0.012 | 0.612 | 0.62 | 0.66 |
| **Logistic, walk-forward, 14-day decay** | **+0.044 / +0.024** | **0.587** | **0.64** | 0.67 |

- **The winner beats the baseline in all 4 test weeks.** I resampled whole days 2,000 times to put an interval on its Brier improvement: +0.031, 95% interval [+0.016, +0.047]. That's clearly above zero, so it isn't noise.
- **The floor made no difference on test.** Every test hour had at least one alert in the previous 24h, so the baseline never predicted exactly 0.
- **The plan was committed before the test ran** (`6ce1942`), so the git history shows the choice wasn't made after seeing the results.

## Why it flipped
**The baseline broke** because it turns "alerts in the last 24h" into a probability with a fixed formula. On test, alerts were almost continuous, so that count stopped separating risky hours from safe ones (ROC-AUC 0.54):
- Hours with only 0–4 alerts in the last 24h were still followed by a new alert 57–83% of the time.
- Calm hours had a *higher* chance of a new alert than hours mid-alert (0.77 vs 0.59). A new alert can only start after an all-clear, and the formula can't represent that.

**The decay model won** because it learns from what actually happened recently, not just from counts. Each day it refits the overall level, and the role of "alert active now" and "time since the last all-clear", on roughly the last 2 weeks of outcomes.

**Why validation pointed the other way:** on validation the escalation had only just started. Escalation hours were 0–1.2% of its training data, so there was almost nothing recent to learn from. By the test period it had 3+ weeks.

**Honest limits:**
- Absolute skill is small: Brier skill +0.04 and ROC-AUC 0.64. In this regime, forecasting a new alert within 3 hours is mostly about getting the level right; ranking individual hours stays hard.
- It's one city and one 4-week test window.
- All the analysis after scoring was descriptive. No model was changed after seeing test results.

## Reflection draft (~100 words) for the submission form
> My first models (logistic regression, LightGBM) were trained once on 2023–Aug 2026 and lost to a simple baseline. LightGBM even ranked hours backwards (AUC 0.36). What went wrong: alerts escalated sharply from 24 August, and trees cannot extrapolate beyond the range they were trained on. I adjusted by retraining every day on labels known by that day, with leak tests that try to make each check fail, and by weighting recent data more (14-day half-life). I froze the plan before testing. The baseline won validation but failed on test, while the adaptive logistic model won all four test weeks. My final version is better because it adapts to regime change.

That's 108 words; edit it into your own voice. One more open item: the repo still needs a GitHub remote, a push, and possibly the author fix I mentioned earlier, before you can submit the link.

  "content": "\"\"\"Download the current Kyiv Digital alert history (public endpoint, no token needed).\"\"\"\nimport hashlib\nimport json\nimport urllib.request\nfrom datetime import datetime, timezone\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nfrom . import config\n\n\ndef validate_payload(raw: bytes) -> int:\n    \"\"\"Check the response looks like the history we analysed; returns the number of events.\"\"\"\n    items = json.loads(raw)[\"items\"]\n    if not items:\n        raise ValueError(\"history response has no events\")\n    for item in items:\n        if set(item) < {\"state\", \"created_at\"} or item[\"state\"] not in (0, 1):\n            raise ValueError(f\"unexpected event format: {item}\")\n    return len(items)\n\n\ndef fetch_history(url: str = config.HISTORY_URL, dest: Path = config.LIVE_JSON, timeout: float = 30) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Save the full history to `dest` (written atomically) plus a small metadata file.\"\"\"\n    request = urllib.request.Request(url, headers={\"Accept\": \"application/json\", \"User-Agent\": \"kse-air-alerts/0.1\"})\n    with urllib.request.urlopen(request, timeout=timeout) as response:\n        raw = response.read()\n    n = validate_payload(raw)\n\n    dest.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    tmp = dest.with_suffix(\".tmp\")\n    tmp.write_bytes(raw)\n    tmp.replace(dest)\n    meta = {\n        \"source_url\": url,\n        \"fetched_at_utc\": datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(timespec=\"seconds\"),\n        \"event_count\": n,\n        \"sha256\": hashlib.sha256(raw).hexdigest(),\n    }\n    meta_path(dest).write_text(json.dumps(meta, indent=2))\n    return meta\n\n\ndef meta_path(dest: Path) -> Path:\n    return dest.with_name(dest.stem + \".meta.json\")\n"

2026-10-15T20:35:43.158Z | ASSISTANT | 07fad9a6-b364-4d66-bc00-08c309eea2ef
Sure, nothing is committed. Here's what to look at, in reading order:

1. [scripts/predict.py](scripts/predict.py), the entry point:
   - `load_source()` decides between a fresh download, the cached download and the pinned snapshot.
   - `main()` sets the forecast time. It uses *now* only with fresh data. Otherwise it uses the download time and prints a warning. A replay time after the data was retrieved is refused.
2. [src/air_alerts/predict.py](src/air_alerts/predict.py), where the model work happens:
   - `forecast_at()` cuts the event log at the forecast time, then refits the final model on every past forecast hour whose 3-hour outcome was already known (t + 3h ≤ now).
   - `describe()` formats the output.
3. [src/air_alerts/fetch.py](src/air_alerts/fetch.py), the download:
   - It checks the response format.
   - It writes the file in one step, so a failed download can't leave a half-written file.
   - It uses `certifi` for HTTPS certificates; certificate checking stays on.
4. [tests/test_predict.py](tests/test_predict.py):
   - A replay must give the same result whether or not the log contains events after the forecast time.
   - Training may only use outcomes known at forecast time.
   - Malformed API responses are rejected.

Smaller changes in existing files:
- `load_events(cutoff=None)` for live data.
- `build_dataset(cutoff=...)`.
- The new `HISTORY_URL`, `LIVE_JSON` and `RAW_RETRIEVED_AT` settings in config.
- `certifi` added to requirements.
- `data/live/` added to `.gitignore`.

Choices you might want to question while reviewing:
- **The model is refit on every run instead of being saved once.** That takes about a second and always uses the latest outcomes. The downside is that a run can't be reproduced later unless you keep that download.
- **Forecasts are issued at the exact minute, not on the hour.** The model was trained only on on-the-hour forecasts. The features change smoothly between hours, so I'd expect this to be fine, but I haven't tested it separately.
- **`RAW_RETRIEVED_AT = 19:59`** is taken from your snapshot file's modification time, not from metadata the API provided.
- **The 2-line test-skill note in the output is hard-coded.** It will go stale if the model changes.

Tell me what you'd like changed.

# Live forecast review

Reviewed the uncommitted changes in:
`/Users/oleksandr/code/KSE/AI-Agentic-School/KSE-AI-Agentic-School-Stage2`

The review and execution used a separate temporary copy. The original working tree was not changed.

## Verification

- All **43 tests pass**.
- A real download and current forecast succeeded.
- Offline forecasting and a historical replay succeeded.

The CLI is functional. Please address the following issues before committing.

## 1. Validate the complete response before replacing the cache [P2]

**Location:** `src/air_alerts/fetch.py:14–22, 32–37`; `scripts/predict.py:29–39`.

`validate_payload()` checks the presence of `created_at`, but does not check that it is a valid timestamp. A payload containing `created_at="not-a-date"` passes validation and replaces the cache. `load_events()` then fails. The exception handler prints a download warning, but the fallback immediately loads the same invalid cache and fails again.

I reproduced this with a mocked HTTP response. The invalid file remained on disk after the failed load.

**Requested fix:** Parse and validate all fields needed by the loader before replacing the last working cache. A failed download or validation must preserve the previous usable cache. Add a regression test for an invalid timestamp and verify that fallback still works.

## 2. Handle a missing or invalid cache metadata file [P2]

**Location:** `scripts/predict.py:36–39`; `src/air_alerts/fetch.py:35–44`.

When the cached JSON exists but its metadata file is missing, `load_source(fetch=False)` raises `FileNotFoundError` instead of falling back to the pinned snapshot. I reproduced this directly.

The JSON replacement is atomic, but the JSON and metadata are not updated as one unit. An interrupted first download can leave a JSON file without metadata; an interrupted later download can leave new JSON paired with old metadata. The stored SHA-256 is not checked when loading the cache.

**Requested fix:** Validate the JSON and metadata together, including the stored hash and retrieval timestamp. If the pair is unusable, warn and try the pinned snapshot. Consider a single atomic cache envelope containing both payload and metadata, or another strategy that prevents mismatched versions. Add tests for missing metadata, malformed metadata, and a hash mismatch.

## 3. Distinguish live behavior from the evaluated protocol [P2]

**Location:** `src/air_alerts/predict.py:35–43, 89–92`.

The published test metrics were obtained with daily refitting at Kyiv midnight and forecasts at hourly timestamps. The new CLI refits at every invocation using all labels known at that moment, and can forecast at arbitrary minutes. This changes both the training cutoff within a day and the forecast grid.

That is not automatically incorrect or leaky, but the hard-coded test metrics do not directly evaluate this exact live procedure.

**Requested fix:** Either preserve the evaluated daily training cutoff for live predictions, or clearly explain that the CLI uses a different refitting schedule. Label the printed metrics as historical results from the daily-refit, hourly evaluation. Do not imply that arbitrary-minute forecasts have been separately validated. Do not tune model settings on the already inspected test period.

## Scope

Please keep the existing model settings and historical evaluation unchanged while fixing cache reliability. The model does not need to be redesigned to address these findings.

[TOOL RESULT: toolu_01SoiJjjEMorso61ayuudSPE]
1	"""Loading the Kyiv Digital event log and turning it into alert episodes."""
2	import hashlib
3	import json
4	from pathlib import Path
5	
6	import numpy as np
7	import pandas as pd
8	
9	from . import config
10	
11	
12	def load_raw(path: Path = config.RAW_JSON, sha256: str | None = config.RAW_SHA256) -> pd.DataFrame:
13	    raw = Path(path).read_bytes()
14	    if sha256 is not None:
15	        digest = hashlib.sha256(raw).hexdigest()
16	        if digest != sha256:
17	            raise ValueError(f"{path}: sha256 {digest} does not match the pinned snapshot {sha256}")
18	    return pd.DataFrame(json.loads(raw)["items"], columns=["created_at", "state", "causes"])
19	
20	
21	def kyiv_local_to_utc(local: pd.Series) -> pd.Series:
22	    """Convert offset-less Kyiv wall-clock strings to UTC.
23	
24	    Spring-forward gaps cannot occur in real data, but are shifted forward to be safe.
25	    In the repeated autumn hour the first occurrence (summer time) is assumed unless that
26	    would put the event before the previous one; `local` must be sorted.
27	    """
28	    naive = pd.to_datetime(local, format="%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S")
29	    utc = (
30	        naive.dt.tz_localize(config.TZ, ambiguous="NaT", nonexistent="shift_forward")
31	        .dt.tz_convert("UTC")
32	    )
33	    for i in np.flatnonzero(utc.isna().to_numpy()):
34	        early = naive.iloc[i].tz_localize(config.TZ, ambiguous=True).tz_convert("UTC")
35	        late = naive.iloc[i].tz_localize(config.TZ, ambiguous=False).tz_convert("UTC")
36	        prev = utc.iloc[i - 1] if i > 0 else pd.NaT
37	        utc.iloc[i] = late if pd.notna(prev) and early < prev else early
38	    return utc
39	
40	
41	def load_events(
42	    path: Path = config.RAW_JSON,
43	    sha256: str | None = config.RAW_SHA256,
44	    cutoff: pd.Timestamp | None = config.CUTOFF,
45	) -> pd.DataFrame:
46	    """Start (state=1) / end (state=0) events with UTC time `t`, sorted, strictly before `cutoff`
47	    (no cutoff if None, for live data).
48	
49	    `causes` is kept for after-the-fact analysis only. It must never become a model input:
50	    the API rewrites a start record's threat types later in the alert (observed: the 19:24 start on
51	    2026-10-15 read ["drone"] at 20:00 and ["drone", "missile"] at 23:30).
52	    """
53	    df = load_raw(path, sha256)
54	    # "YYYY-mm-dd HH:MM:SS" sorts chronologically as a string.
55	    df = df.sort_values("created_at", kind="stable").reset_index(drop=True)
56	    df["t"] = kyiv_local_to_utc(df["created_at"])
57	    if cutoff is not None:
58	        df = df[df["t"] < cutoff.tz_convert("UTC")].reset_index(drop=True)
59	    return df[["t", "state", "causes", "created_at"]]
60

Raises CacheError if any part is unusable.\"\"\"\n    try:\n        envelope = json.loads(path.read_bytes())\n        if envelope.get(\"version\") != CACHE_VERSION:\n            raise ValueError(f\"unknown cache version {envelope.get('version')!r}\")\n        raw = envelope[\"response\"].encode(\"utf-8\")\n        if hashlib.sha256(raw).hexdigest() != envelope[\"sha256\"]:\n            raise ValueError(\"sha256 does not match the stored response\")\n        fetched_at = pd.Timestamp(envelope[\"fetched_at_utc\"])\n        if fetched_at.tzinfo is None or fetched_at > pd.Timestamp.now(tz=\"UTC\") + CLOCK_SLACK:\n            raise ValueError(f\"implausible retrieval time {envelope['fetched_at_utc']!r}\")\n        return parse_events(raw), fetched_at.tz_convert(\"UTC\")\n    except (OSError, ValueError, KeyError, TypeError, AttributeError) as exc:\n        raise CacheError(f\"{path}: {exc}\") from exc\n"
}

1	"""Forecast whether a new air-raid alert starts in Kyiv City within the next 3 hours.
2	
3	    python scripts/predict.py                          # download fresh data, forecast for now
4	    python scripts/predict.py --offline                # use the last downloaded data
5	    python scripts/predict.py --at "2026-10-10 18:00"  # replay a past moment (Kyiv time)
6	
7	Fresh data is saved to data/live/ (not in git). Offline and replay modes use that file if it
8	exists, otherwise the pinned snapshot in data/raw/. Without fresh data the forecast is issued as
9	of the moment the data was retrieved, not "now": the current alert status is unknown after that.
10	"""
11	import argparse
12	import json
13	import sys
14	from pathlib import Path
15	
16	import pandas as pd
17	
18	sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1] / "src"))
19	
20	from air_alerts import config  # noqa: E402
21	from air_alerts.data import load_events  # noqa: E402
22	from air_alerts.fetch import fetch_history, meta_path  # noqa: E402
23	from air_alerts.predict import describe, forecast_at  # noqa: E402
24	
25	
26	def load_source(fetch: bool) -> tuple[pd.DataFrame, pd.Timestamp, str, bool]:
27	    """Event log, the moment it was retrieved, a note on its origin, and whether it is fresh."""
28	    if fetch:
29	        try:
30	            meta = fetch_history()
31	            events = load_events(config.LIVE_JSON, sha256=None, cutoff=None)
32	            return events, pd.Timestamp(meta["fetched_at_utc"]), f"downloaded just now ({meta['event_count']} events)", True
33	        except Exception as exc:  # network, HTTP or format problems
34	            print(f"WARNING: download failed ({exc})\n", file=sys.stderr)
35	
36	    if config.LIVE_JSON.exists():
37	        fetched = pd.Timestamp(json.loads(meta_path(config.LIVE_JSON).read_text())["fetched_at_utc"])
38	        events = load_events(config.LIVE_JSON, sha256=None, cutoff=None)
39	        return events, fetched, f"cached download from {kyiv(fetched)} Kyiv", False
40	    events = load_events(cutoff=None)
41	    return events, config.RAW_RETRIEVED_AT, f"pinned snapshot {config.RAW_JSON.relative_to(config.ROOT)}", False
42	
43	
44	def kyiv(ts: pd.Timestamp) -> str:
45	    return ts.tz_convert(config.TZ).strftime("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M")
46	
47	
48	def main() -> None:
49	    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__, formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter)
50	    parser.add_argument("--offline", action="store_true", help="do not download; use cached or pinned data")
51	    parser.add_argument("--at", help='replay a past moment, Kyiv time, e.g. "2026-10-10 18:00"')
52	    args = parser.parse_args()
53	
54	    events, retrieved_at, source, fresh = load_source(fetch=not (args.offline or args.at))
55	    if args.at:
56	        at = pd.Timestamp(args.at, tz=config.TZ)
57	        if at > retrieved_at:
58	            sys.exit(f"--at {args.at} is after the data was retrieved ({kyiv(retrieved_at)} Kyiv); "
59	                     "run without --offline/--at to download fresh data")
60	    elif fresh:
61	        at = pd.Timestamp.now(tz="UTC")
62	    else:
63	        at = retrieved_at
64	        age_h = (pd.Timestamp.now(tz="UTC") - retrieved_at) / pd.Timedelta(hours=1)
65	        print(f"WARNING: no fresh data. Forecast is issued as of {kyiv(at)} Kyiv, when the data was\n"
66	              f"retrieved ({age_h:.1f} h ago). The alert status after that moment is unknown.\n")
67	    if at < config.DATA_START + pd.Timedelta(days=30):
68	        sys.exit("--at must be at least 30 days after 2023-01-01 so the model has history to learn from")
69	
70	    print(describe(forecast_at(events, at)))
71	    print(f"\n  Data: {source}")
72	
73	
74	if __name__ == "__main__":
75	    main()
76

2026-10-15T20:44:23.755Z | USER | 22379500-e7b2-4cf0-83d5-678b20a5ef29

1	"""Forecast for a single moment: P(a new alert starts in Kyiv within the next 3 hours).
2	
3	Uses the final model chosen on validation and confirmed on test: logistic regression with
4	14-day time-decay weights, refit at forecast time on every label already observed. The event
5	log is cut at the forecast time first, so a replay of a past moment sees only what had been
6	published by then.
7	"""
8	from dataclasses import dataclass
9	
10	import numpy as np
11	import pandas as pd
12	
13	from . import config
14	from .baselines import FlooredRecentRate24h
15	from .data import build_episodes
16	from .dataset import build_dataset
17	from .features import make_features
18	from .models import DECAY_MODELS
19	
20	FINAL_MODEL = "logistic+decay14"
21	
22	
23	@dataclass
24	class Forecast:
25	    issued_at: pd.Timestamp          # UTC
26	    probability: float
27	    baseline_probability: float      # 24h recent-rate baseline, for reference
28	    recent_base_rate: float          # share of 3h windows with a new start, last 14 days
29	    features: pd.Series
30	    latest_event: pd.Series | None   # newest event at or before issued_at
31	    train_rows: int
32	    train_until: pd.Timestamp        # newest training forecast time (label known by issued_at)
33	
34	
35	def forecast_at(events: pd.DataFrame, at: pd.Timestamp, model: str = FINAL_MODEL) -> Forecast:
36	    at = at.tz_convert("UTC")
37	    events = events[events["t"] <= at]  # nothing published after the forecast time
38	    episodes, _ = build_episodes(events)
39	
40	    history = build_dataset(events, cutoff=at)
41	    fitted = DECAY_MODELS[model]().fit(history)
42	
43	    x = make_features(pd.DatetimeIndex([at]), episodes)
44	    recent = history[history.index > history.index.max() - pd.Timedelta(days=14)]
45	    return Forecast(
46	        issued_at=at,
47	        probability=float(fitted.predict(x)[0]),
48	        baseline_probability=float(FlooredRecentRate24h().predict(x)[0]),
49	        recent_base_rate=float(recent["y"].mean()),
50	        features=x.iloc[0],
51	        latest_event=events.iloc[-1] if len(events) else None,
52	        train_rows=len(history),
53	        train_until=history.index.max(),
54	    )
55	
56	
57	def describe(fc: Forecast) -> str:
58	    """Human-readable summary in Kyiv time."""
59	    def kyiv(ts: pd.Timestamp) -> str:
60	        return ts.tz_convert(config.TZ).strftime("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M")
61	
62	    f = fc.features
63	    until = fc.issued_at + config.HORIZON
64	    if f["active_now"]:
65	        status = f"ALERT ACTIVE for {f['current_alert_min']:.0f} min"
66	    elif np.isnan(f["min_since_last_end"]):
67	        status = "no alert"
68	    else:
69	        status = f"no alert; last all-clear {f['min_since_last_end']:.0f} min ago"
70	    if fc.latest_event is not None:
71	        kind = "start" if fc.latest_event["state"] == 1 else "all-clear"
72	        latest = f"{kyiv(fc.latest_event['t'])} ({kind})"
73	    else:
74	        latest = "none"
75	
76	    return "\n".join([
77	        f"Kyiv City air-alert forecast, issued {kyiv(fc.issued_at)} Kyiv time",
78	        "",
79	        f"  P(new alert starts by {kyiv(until)}):  {fc.probability:.0%}",
80	        "",
81	        f"  Status now:            {status}",
82	        f"  Alert starts:          {f['starts_3h']:.0f} in last 3h, {f['starts_24h']:.0f} in last 24h, {f['starts_7d']:.0f} in last 7 days",
83	        f"  Latest event in data:  {latest}",
84	        "",
85	        "  For comparison:",
86	        f"    24h-rate baseline:                      {fc.baseline_probability:.0%}",
87	        f"    3h windows with a new start, last 14d:  {fc.recent_base_rate:.0%}",
88	        "",
89	        f"  Model: logistic regression, 14-day time-decay, refit now on {fc.train_rows} hourly forecasts",
90	        f"  whose outcome is known (latest issued {kyiv(fc.train_until)}).",
91	        "  Test-period skill (17 Sep - 14 Oct 2026): Brier skill +0.04, ROC-AUC 0.64 - mostly right on",
92	        "  the overall level, weak at telling individual hours apart. Not an official warning.",
93	    ])
94

  "content": "\"\"\"Do forecasts issued at arbitrary minutes score like the evaluated on-the-hour forecasts?\n\nThe model is trained on forecasts issued on the hour, but scripts/predict.py forecasts at any\nminute. noqa: E402\nfrom air_alerts.labels import label_new_start  # noqa: E402\nfrom air_alerts.models import DECAY_MODELS  # noqa: E402\nfrom air_alerts.predict import FINAL_MODEL  # noqa: E402\nfrom air_alerts.walkforward import known_by  # noqa: E402\n\n\ndef main() -> None:\n    events = load_events()\n    episodes, _ = build_episodes(events)\n    hourly = build_dataset(events)\n    test_hours = hourly.index[hourly[\"split\"] == \"test\"]\n\n    rng = np.random.default_rng(0)\n    shifted = test_hours + pd.to_timedelta(rng.integers(0, 60, len(test_hours)), unit=\"min\")\n    keep = shifted + config.HORIZON <= config.CUTOFF  # label window must lie inside the data\n    variants = {\"on the hour\": test_hours[keep], \"random minute\": shifted[keep]}\n\n    rows = []\n    for name, times in variants.items():\n        x = make_features(times, episodes)\n        y = label_new_start(times, episodes[\"start\"]).astype(int)\n        p = np.empty(len(times))\n        local_day = times.tz_convert(config.TZ).normalize()\n        for day in local_day.unique():\n            model = DECAY_MODELS[FINAL_MODEL]().fit(known_by(hourly, day))\n            mask = np.asarray(local_day == day)\n            p[mask] = model.predict(x[mask])\n        rows.append({\"forecast times\": name, **score(y, p)})\n\n    with pd.option_context(\"display.width\", 160, \"display.float_format\", \"{:.3f}\".format):\n        print(f\"Test period, model {FINAL_MODEL}, daily refit at Kyiv midnight\")\n        print(pd.DataFrame(rows).set_index(\"forecast times\").to_string())\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\n"

# Full project review

**Repository:** `/Users/oleksandr/code/KSE/AI-Agentic-School/KSE-AI-Agentic-School-Stage2`

**Reviewed revision:** `9b1ed276ccf4574bf984a968459c11993e56dc27` (working tree clean at review start).

**Review date:** 15 October 2026.

Reviewed all source modules, CLI scripts, tests, dependency declarations, README, decision log, and saved evaluation reports. Execution and failure reproductions used a separate temporary copy; the original repository was not changed.

## Summary

The pinned historical experiment runs successfully and its main test metrics reproduce. The previous review's malformed-date handling, single-file cache, and daily-refit fixes are present. The model settings do not need to be retuned.

The broader review found **four remaining P2 issues**: incorrect ordering across the autumn clock change, acceptance of unusable partial histories into the live cache, incorrect loading when extending the dataset cutoff, and an unsupported Python version advertised by the project.

## Verified behavior

- **58 tests passed** under Python 3.12 with the pinned dependencies.
- Test evaluation reproduced the reported values:

  | Forecaster | Brier | Brier skill | Log loss | ROC-AUC |
  |---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
  | 24h baseline | 0.231 | -0.105 | 0.658 | 0.537 |
  | Daily-refit logistic, 14-day decay | 0.200 | +0.044 | 0.587 | 0.643 |

- The existing tests include historical truncation, observed-label cutoffs, corrupted-cache fallback, and equality between hourly live predictions and daily walk-forward predictions.
- No additional future-event leakage was identified in the normal feature calculations or daily training cutoff during this review. This is scoped to the implemented event-time assumptions, not proof that the upstream API never revises its history.

## Findings

### 1. Preserve chronological order across the repeated autumn hour [P2]

**Locations:** `src/air_alerts/data.py:21–38, 61–65`; `tests/test_data.py:28–35`.

`parse_events()` sorts offset-less local timestamp strings before resolving daylight-saving ambiguity. Local clock order is not chronological order when clocks go back. This destroys the ordering information needed to distinguish the two occurrences of the repeated hour. The subsequent converter generally picks the summer-time occurrence, so it cannot recover an alert that spans the clock change.

**Reproduced input**, in the API's usual newest-first order:

```json
{"items": [
  {"state": 0, "created_at": "2025-10-26 03:10:00"},
  {"state": 1, "created_at": "2025-10-26 03:50:00"}
]}
```

If the start is in the first occurrence of the hour and the end is in the second, the correct UTC interval is **00:50–01:10**, lasting 20 minutes. The current parser instead produces:

```text
2025-10-26 00:10 UTC  state=0
2025-10-26 00:50 UTC  state=1
```

`build_episodes()` then reports `end_while_clear` and leaves the alert open. This corrupts active status, durations, and labels around the transition. The current DST test only supplies ascending local times and does not exercise an actual backward clock transition.

**Requested fix:** Resolve ambiguity before discarding trusted source ordering. If the API does not guarantee usable ordering, document that limitation and reject or explicitly flag ambiguous cases rather than silently inventing an open episode. Add a test covering a start in the first repeated hour and an end in the second. Do not claim the existing single ambiguous historical event has been independently resolved unless there is supporting evidence.

### 2. A syntactically valid but unusable history can still replace the good cache [P2]

**Locations:** `src/air_alerts/fetch.py:38–55, 90–103`; `src/air_alerts/predict.py:40–45`; `src/air_alerts/features.py:49–57`.

The revised parser correctly rejects malformed dates, but cache acceptance still checks only row structure and timestamp parsing. It does not ensure that the response contains usable historical model input. A partial response containing only a valid all-clear is accepted, atomically replaces the cache, and is accepted again by `load_cache()`.

**Reproduced payload:**

```json
{"items": [{"state": 0, "created_at": "2026-10-15 10:30:00"}]}
```

`save_cache()` and `load_cache()` both succeed. `forecast_at()` then fails with:

```text
IndexError: index 0 is out of bounds for axis 0 with size 0
```

There are no episodes, so feature construction indexes an empty array. The source was already marked fresh and forecasting happens outside the fallback handler, so the pinned snapshot is not used. A short response that does contain a start is also dangerous: the fixed 2023 forecast grid can turn unobserved earlier history into years of negative labels.

**Requested fix:** Add a separate usability/coverage check for the full-history forecasting source before replacing the last good cache or treating it as usable. Require enough historical coverage and valid model-training outcomes; do not interpret absent history as confirmed absence of alerts. Keep generic parsing separate if short event logs are useful in unit tests. Route unusable sources through fallback or give a clear error. Add regression tests for an end-only response and a truncated recent-only history.

### 3. `build_dataset(cutoff=...)` can silently extend labels beyond its loaded data [P2]

**Location:** `src/air_alerts/dataset.py:10–18`.

When callers omit `events`, the function calls `load_events()` with its default fixed `config.CUTOFF`, even if a different dataset cutoff was supplied. It then creates rows through the caller's later cutoff and labels them from a log already truncated at the old boundary.

**Reproduced on the actual pinned snapshot:**

```python
cutoff = config.kyiv("2026-10-15 19:00")
implicit = build_dataset(cutoff=cutoff)
explicit = build_dataset(load_events(cutoff=None), cutoff=cutoff)
```

There are **17 rows** issued at or after the original midnight cutoff. Their labels disagree in **12 rows**: the implicit call has discarded the events needed to label them.

The current live CLI passes events explicitly, so its ordinary path is not affected. The new public function argument is nevertheless unsafe for the natural use case of extending or refreshing the experiment.

**Requested fix:** Load events consistently with the requested cutoff and verify that the requested observation window is supported by the source. Preserve the existing default historical results. Add a regression test comparing implicit and explicit loading for an extended cutoff within the snapshot's actual coverage; reject extensions beyond known coverage rather than manufacturing negative outcomes.

### 4. Python 3.11 is advertised but cannot install the pinned dependencies [P2]

**Locations:** `pyproject.toml:5`; `requirements.txt:1`; README setup instructions.

The project declares `requires-python = ">=3.11"`, but pinned `numpy==2.5.4` declares **`Requires-Python: >=3.12`**. I verified this from the installed wheel metadata. A user following setup with Python 3.11 cannot install the required NumPy version.

**Requested fix:** If preserving the evaluated dependency set, declare Python >=3.12 and explicitly tell users to create the environment with Python 3.12 or later. Alternatively, select and verify a NumPy version supporting 3.11. Prefer the documentation/version correction over changing numerical dependencies just before submission.

## Reporting and reproducibility follow-ups

These are separate from the four functional findings above:

- **Correct the model-selection description.** `src/air_alerts/predict.py:3` says the deployed model was “chosen on validation and confirmed on test.” The decision log correctly says the primary chosen on validation was the baseline, while logistic was the best learned comparator and performed best on test. Keep that distinction in the CLI documentation and reflection. Selecting logistic for the demo after this comparison is understandable, but it is not the pre-registered primary result and has no new untouched evaluation afterward.
- **Make the confidence-interval claim reproducible.** README and DECISIONS quote a day-bootstrap Brier improvement interval of `[+0.016, +0.047]`, but the checked-in scripts only reproduce aggregate scores and minute-offset scores. Add a fixed-seed analysis script and/or the per-forecast predictions and exact bootstrap procedure, or omit the unreproducible interval from the main result. State that it is a conditional uncertainty estimate for this short observed period, not proof of performance under future regimes.
- **Avoid overstating cause.** The logs establish an increase in recorded alert starts before the format change. They do not by themselves prove the increase was caused solely by real attack escalation. Phrase that as interpretation unless supported by independent evidence.
- **Validation is meant for model selection.** The comment at the top of `models.py` incorrectly says tuning on validation turns it into training data. Repeated extensive tuning can overfit validation, but limited declared selection on validation is normal; the final evaluation must remain separate.

## Suggested order

1. Fix the Python requirement and cutoff propagation.
2. Add coverage checks before accepting a new full-history cache.
3. Resolve or explicitly flag autumn-hour ambiguity and add the missing transition test.
4. Correct reporting language and make the statistical claims reproducible.
5. Rerun the suite and confirm that the pinned historical metrics remain unchanged, except for any justified timestamp correction that is explicitly documented.

Do not retune models to improve the already inspected test scores. These fixes concern correctness, reliability, and faithful reporting.

50	def payload(*events: tuple[str, int]) -> bytes:
51	    return json.dumps({"items": [{"state": s, "causes": [], "created_at": c} for c, s in events]}).encode()
52	
53	
54	GOOD = payload(("2026-10-15 10:00:00", 1), ("2026-10-15 10:30:00", 0))
55	FETCHED = pd.Timestamp("2026-10-15 08:00", tz="UTC")
56	
57	
58	@pytest.fixture
59	def cache(tmp_path):
60	    path = tmp_path / "cache.json"
61	    save_cache(GOOD, FETCHED, path)
62	    return path
63	
64	
65	@pytest.mark.parametrize("bad", [
66	    payload(("not-a-date", 1)),
67	    payload(("2026-10-15 10:00", 1)),          # missing seconds
68	    payload(("2026-10-15 10:00:00", 2)),
69	    json.dumps({"items": [{"state": 1, "time": "2026-10-15 10:00:00"}]}).encode(),
70	    json.dumps({"items": []}).encode(),
71	    json.dumps({"data": []}).encode(),
72	    b"<html>maintenance</html>",
73	])
74	def test_invalid_download_never_replaces_good_cache(cache, bad):
75	    before = cache.read_bytes()
76	    with pytest.raises(ValueError):
77	        save_cache(bad, FETCHED, cache)
78	    assert cache.read_bytes() == before
79	    assert not list(cache.parent.glob("*.tmp"))
80	    events, fetched = load_cache(cache)
81	    assert len(events) == 2 and fetched == FETCHED
82	
83	
84	def test_failed_download_falls_back_to_cache(cache, monkeypatch):
85	    monkeypatch.setattr(fetch, "download", lambda *a, **k: payload(("not-a-date", 1)))
86	    src = load_latest(fetch=True, cache=cache)
87	    assert not src.fresh and src.retrieved_at == FETCHED and len(src.events) == 2
88	    assert "download failed" in src.warnings[0]
89	
90	    def offline(*a, **k):
91	        raise urllib.error.URLError("no network")
92	    monkeypatch.setattr(fetch, "download", offline)
93	    src = load_latest(fetch=True, cache=cache)
94	    assert not src.fresh and len(src.events) == 2
95	
96	
97	def test_successful_download_replaces_cache(cache, monkeypatch):
98	    newer = payload(("2026-10-15 10:00:00", 1), ("2026-10-15 10:30:00", 0), ("2026-10-15 11:00:00", 1))
99	    monkeypatch.setattr(fetch, "download", lambda *a, **k: newer)
100	    src = load_latest(fetch=True, cache=cache)
101	    assert src.fresh and len(src.events) == 3 and not src.warnings
102	    assert len(load_cache(cache)[0]) == 3
103

105	def corrupt(path, change):
106	    envelope = json.loads(path.read_text())
107	    change(envelope)
108	    path.write_text(json.dumps(envelope))
109	
110	
111	@pytest.mark.parametrize("change", [
112	    lambda e: e.update(response=e["response"].replace("10:30:00", "10:31:00")),  # hash mismatch
113	    lambda e: e.pop("sha256"),
114	    lambda e: e.pop("fetched_at_utc"),
115	    lambda e: e.update(fetched_at_utc="yesterday"),
116	    lambda e: e.update(fetched_at_utc="2026-10-15T08:00:00"),                  # no time zone
117	    lambda e: e.update(fetched_at_utc="2999-01-01T00:00:00+00:00"),            # in the future
118	    lambda e: e.update(version=99),
119	], ids=["hash", "no-hash", "no-time", "bad-time", "naive-time", "future-time", "version"])
120	def test_unusable_cache_falls_back_to_pinned_snapshot(cache, change):
121	    corrupt(cache, change)
122	    with pytest.raises(CacheError):
123	        load_cache(cache)
124	    src = load_latest(fetch=False, cache=cache)
125	    assert src.note.startswith("pinned snapshot") and "cached data unusable" in src.warnings[0]
126	    assert src.retrieved_at == config.RAW_RETRIEVED_AT
127

129	@pytest.mark.parametrize("content", [b"", b"{not json", b"[]"])
130	def test_truncated_or_garbled_cache_falls_back(tmp_path, content):
131	    path = tmp_path / "cache.json"
132	    path.write_bytes(content)
133	    src = load_latest(fetch=False, cache=path)
134	    assert src.note.startswith("pinned snapshot") and src.warnings

20	
21	def kyiv_local_to_utc(local: pd.Series) -> pd.Series:
22	    """Convert offset-less Kyiv wall-clock strings to UTC.
23	
24	    Spring-forward gaps cannot occur in real data, but are shifted forward to be safe.
25	    In the repeated autumn hour the first occurrence (summer time) is assumed unless that
26	    would put the event before the previous one; `local` must be sorted.
27	    """
28	    naive = pd.to_datetime(local, format="%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S")
29	    utc = (
30	        naive.dt.tz_localize(config.TZ, ambiguous="NaT", nonexistent="shift_forward")
31	        .dt.tz_convert("UTC")
32	    )
33	    for i in np.flatnonzero(utc.isna().to_numpy()):
34	        early = naive.iloc[i].tz_localize(config.TZ, ambiguous=True).tz_convert("UTC")
35	        late = naive.iloc[i].tz_localize(config.TZ, ambiguous=False).tz_convert("UTC")
36	        prev = utc.iloc[i - 1] if i > 0 else pd.NaT
37	        utc.iloc[i] = late if pd.notna(prev) and early < prev else early
38	    return utc
39	
40	
41	def parse_events(raw: bytes, cutoff: pd.Timestamp | None = None) -> pd.DataFrame:
42	    """Start (state=1) / end (state=0) events with UTC time `t`, sorted, strictly before `cutoff`
43	    (no cutoff if None, for live data). Raises ValueError on anything the pipeline can't use.
44	
45	    `causes` is kept for after-the-fact analysis only. It must never become a model input:
46	    the API rewrites a start record's threat types later in the alert (observed: the 19:24 start on
47	    2026-10-15 read ["drone"] at 20:00 and ["drone", "missile"] at 23:30).
48	    """
49	    try:
50	        items = json.loads(raw)["items"]
51	    except (ValueError, KeyError, TypeError) as exc:
52	        raise ValueError(f"not an alert-history response: {exc!r}") from exc
53	    if not isinstance(items, list) or not items:
54	        raise ValueError("alert history has no events")
55	    for item in items:
56	        if not isinstance(item, dict) or not {"state", "created_at"} <= set(item):
57	            raise ValueError(f"event without state/created_at: {item!r}")
58	        if item["state"] not in (0, 1) or isinstance(item["state"], bool) or not isinstance(item["created_at"], str):
59	            raise ValueError(f"malformed event: {item!r}")
60	
61	    df = pd.DataFrame(items).reindex(columns=["created_at", "state", "causes"])
62	    # "YYYY-mm-dd HH:MM:SS" sorts chronologically as a string; the strict format in
63	    # kyiv_local_to_utc rejects anything else.
64	    df = df.sort_values("created_at", kind="stable").reset_index(drop=True)
65	    df["t"] = kyiv_local_to_utc(df["created_at"])
66	    if df["t"].isna().any():
67	        raise ValueError("unparseable created_at")
68	    if cutoff is not None:
69	        df = df[df["t"] < cutoff.tz_convert("UTC")].reset_index(drop=True)
70	    return df[["t", "state", "causes", "created_at"]]
71	
72

Day-block bootstrap CI.\"\"\"\n    by_day = [np.flatnonzero(days == d) for d in np.unique(days)]\n    loss_ref, loss_new = (p_ref - y) ** 2, (p_new - y) ** 2\n    reps = np.empty(REPS)\n    for r in range(REPS):\n        idx = np.concatenate([by_day[k] for k in rng.integers(0, len(by_day), len(by_day))])\n        reps[r] = loss_ref[idx].mean() - loss_new[idx].mean()\n    return loss_ref.mean() - loss_new.mean(), np.percentile(reps, [2.5, 97.5]), (reps > 0).mean()\n\n\ndef bss(y, p):\n    return 1 - np.mean((p - y) ** 2) / (y.mean() * (1 - y.mean()))\n\n\ndef main() -> None:\n    df = build_dataset()\n    test = df[df[\"split\"] == \"test\"]\n    y = test[\"y\"].to_numpy()\n    preds = {\n        \"baseline_24h\": FlooredRecentRate24h().predict(test),\n        \"lightgbm_wf\": walk_forward_predict(df, MODELS[\"lightgbm\"], config.TEST_START, config.CUTOFF).reindex(test.index).to_numpy(),\n        \"logistic_decay14_wf\": walk_forward_predict(df, DECAY_MODELS[\"logistic+decay14\"], config.TEST_START, config.CUTOFF).reindex(test.index).to_numpy(),\n    }\n    local = test.index.tz_convert(config.TZ)\n    days = np.asarray(local.normalize())\n\n    rng = np.random.default_rng(SEED)\n    print(f\"Brier gain on test (positive = second is better); day-block bootstrap, \"\n          f\"{len(np.unique(days))} days, {REPS} reps, seed {SEED}\")\n    for ref, new in [(\"baseline_24h\", \"logistic_decay14_wf\"), (\"baseline_24h\", \"lightgbm_wf\"),\n                     (\"lightgbm_wf\", \"logistic_decay14_wf\")]:\n        gain, (lo, hi), share = bootstrap_brier_gain(y, preds[ref], preds[new], days, rng)\n        print(f\"  {new} vs {ref}: {gain:+.4f}  95% CI [{lo:+.4f}, {hi:+.4f}]  share of reps > 0: {share:.2f}\")\n\n    week = np.asarray((local - config.TEST_START).days // 7)\n    print(\"\\nWeekly Brier skill vs that week's own base rate\")\n    for w in np.unique(week):\n        m = week == w\n        cells = \"  \".join(f\"{k} {bss(y[m], p[m]):+.3f}\" for k, p in preds.items())\n        print(f\"  week {w + 1}: base rate {y[m].mean():.2f}  {cells}\")\n\n    bins = pd.cut(test[\"starts_24h\"], [-1, 2, 4, 6, 9, 12, 40])\n    table = pd.DataFrame({\"y\": y, **preds}, index=test.index).groupby(bins, observed=True)\n    print(\"\\nObserved vs mean predicted, by alert starts in the last 24h\")\n    print(table.agg([\"mean\"]).droplevel(1, axis=1).assign(hours=table.size()).round(2).to_string())\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\n"
}

"""Learned models with the same fit/predict interface as the baselines.

Hyperparameters are fixed up front, not tuned on validation: there are only 574 validation
hours, and tuning on them would turn validation into training data.
"""
from functools import partial
"""Forecast for a single moment: P(a new alert starts in Kyiv within the next 3 hours).

Uses the final model chosen on validation and confirmed on test: logistic regression with
14-day time-decay weights. It follows the evaluated walk-forward protocol exactly: the model is
- **Time zone:** `created_at` has no offset. Matching starts against UTC sources: Europe/Kyiv with DST matches 66–67% in both summer and winter; fixed UTC+2/+3 fail in one season each, UTC fails always. The repeated autumn hour is resolved by keeping the log ordered (one event affected).
- The alert rate jumped on **2026-08-24, two weeks before the new format** (1.7 → 7.6 starts/day). That is real escalation, not an artifact of the new levels.
- **The best learned model won on test**, and robustly: its Brier improvement over the baseline is +0.031, with a 95% day-block bootstrap CI of [+0.016, +0.047] over 28 days. It beats the baseline in all 4 weeks. Its improvement over walk-forward LightGBM is +0.010, CI [+0.000, +0.019].
- **Why the decay model wins:** it learns from recent *outcomes*, not only counts. Every day it refits the level and the role of `active_now` / time since the last all-clear on roughly the last 2 weeks. On validation this was hidden because the escalation had just begun (0–1.2% of the training rows); by the test period it had 3+ weeks of escalation to learn from.
- **Absolute skill is small** (BSS +0.04, AUC 0.64). In this regime, forecasting 3h onsets is mostly about getting the level right; ranking individual hours remains hard.
- Post-hoc analysis above is descriptive only; no model was changed after scoring the test.

## Live forecasting (`scripts/predict.py`)

## Result (test, 17 Sep – 14 Oct 2026, scored once)
Best: **walk-forward logistic regression with 14-day time-decay weights**: Brier skill +0.04 vs the period's base rate, log loss 0.587, ROC-AUC 0.64. It beats the 24h recent-rate baseline (Brier skill −0.10) in all 4 weeks; bootstrap 95% CI for the Brier gain [+0.016, +0.047]. The baseline had been chosen as primary on validation, before the test was scored. The full story is in [DECISIONS.md](DECISIONS.md).

A start at 03:50 (first pass) and an end at 03:10 (second pass) became an end before a start. Now the API's own newest-first order is kept (verified: no exception in 5,386 events). An event is placed in the second pass only when the first would put it before the previous event, and any remaining inconsistency is rejected instead of patched. Events the order can't decide are flagged `dst_assumed`. The snapshot has exactly one: the all-clear at 2025-10-26 03:05:12 (alert started 02:27). Missing history must not be read as \"no alerts\", because it becomes negative labels. Now `check_coverage` runs before a download replaces the cache and when the cache is loaded. The history must reach back before 2022-12-25 (training start minus the 7-day feature window), and it must contain ≥ 99% of the pinned snapshot's alert starts over the snapshot's period. Tests cover end-only, recent-only and 5%-of-starts-missing payloads.\n3. **`build_dataset(cutoff=...)` without events** loaded the snapshot cut at the default cutoff, then labelled later rows from that truncated log: 17 rows, 12 wrong labels for a 19:00 cutoff. <pasted_content id="4baa">
# Follow-up review of the full-project fixes

The DST ordering fix, dataset cutoff propagation, Python requirement correction, and reporting updates are present. One case remains in the partial-history cache safeguard.

## [P2] Validate usable episodes, not only the number of start rows

**Location:** `src/air_alerts/fetch.py`, `check_coverage()` and `save_cache()`.

The coverage check requires old history and enough `state=1` rows. It does not check that all-clear events are present or that the event stream can produce usable training outcomes.

I reproduced this using the real pinned snapshot with every `state=0` row removed:

```python
items = json.loads(config.RAW_JSON.read_bytes())["items"]
raw = json.dumps({"items": [x for x in items if x["state"] == 1]}).encode()
events = save_cache(raw, config.RAW_RETRIEVED_AT, temporary_cache_path)
forecast_at(events, config.RAW_RETRIEVED_AT)
```

Observed:

```text
STARTS_ONLY_ACCEPTED 2713 cache_written True
FORECAST_FAILED ValueError: This solver needs samples of at least 2 classes
in the data, but the data contains only one class: np.int64(0)
```

All starts pass the count check, but `build_episodes()` turns them into one permanently open episode and treats subsequent starts as anomalies. This removes all new onsets after the training start. The bad response has already replaced the last good cache before forecasting fails.

Please check episode usability and substantial loss of all-clear records before accepting a full-history download. Permit the known 2022 anomalies and a final open alert; do not require every historical row to alternate perfectly. Compare actual event coverage with the pinned reference rather than only counting starts, and reject a history that cannot produce both training classes before replacing the cache. Apply the same check when reading cached history.

Add a regression test that removes all all-clear rows and asserts that the previous good cache is preserved and fallback forecasting remains usable.

The new bootstrap script and reporting corrections were inspected. Mutation testing and byte-identical report claims from the author's message have not been independently rerun in this follow-up; the earlier full review reproduced the original metrics.

64	def describe(fc: Forecast) -> str:
65	    """Human-readable summary in Kyiv time."""
66	    def kyiv(ts: pd.Timestamp) -> str:
67	        return ts.tz_convert(config.TZ).strftime("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M")
68	
69	    f = fc.features
70	    until = fc.issued_at + config.HORIZON
71	    if f["active_now"]:
72	        status = f"ALERT ACTIVE for {f['current_alert_min']:.0f} min"
73	    elif np.isnan(f["min_since_last_end"]):
74	        status = "no alert"
75	    else:
76	        status = f"no alert; last all-clear {f['min_since_last_end']:.0f} min ago"
77	    if fc.latest_event is not None:
78	        kind = "start" if fc.latest_event["state"] == 1 else "all-clear"

80	    else:
81	        latest = "none"
82

91	        "",

2026-10-15T21:26:45.095Z | USER | 35e88275-76ba-4bee-8a6c-31f6b4e4b628
[TOOL RESULT: toolu_0147cH3Mc1ehLtzGhv4CConG]
1	"""Forecast whether a new air-raid alert starts in Kyiv City within the next 3 hours.
2	
3	    python scripts/predict.py                          # download fresh data, forecast for now
4	    python scripts/predict.py --offline                # use the last downloaded data
5	    python scripts/predict.py --at "2026-10-10 18:00"  # replay a past moment (Kyiv time)
6	
7	Fresh data is cached in data/live/ (not in git) only after it parses completely. Offline and
8	replay modes use that cache if it is intact, otherwise the pinned snapshot in data/raw/. Without
9	fresh data the forecast is issued as of the moment the data was retrieved, not "now": the alert
10	status after that moment is unknown.
11	"""
12	import argparse
13	import sys
14	from pathlib import Path
15	
16	import pandas as pd
17	
18	sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1] / "src"))
19	
20	from air_alerts import config  # noqa: E402
21	from air_alerts.fetch import load_latest  # noqa: E402
22	from air_alerts.predict import describe, forecast_at  # noqa: E402
23	
24	
25	def kyiv(ts: pd.Timestamp) -> str:
26	    return ts.tz_convert(config.TZ).strftime("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M")
27	
28	
29	def main() -> None:
30	    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__, formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter)
31	    parser.add_argument("--offline", action="store_true", help="do not download; use cached or pinned data")
32	    parser.add_argument("--at", help='replay a past moment, Kyiv time, e.g. "2026-10-10 18:00"')
33	    args = parser.parse_args()
34	
35	    source = load_latest(fetch=not (args.offline or args.at))
36	    for warning in source.warnings:
37	        print(f"WARNING: {warning}\n", file=sys.stderr)
38	    events, retrieved_at = source.events, source.retrieved_at
39	    if args.at:
40	        at = pd.Timestamp(args.at, tz=config.TZ)
41	        if at > retrieved_at:
42	            sys.exit(f"--at {args.at} is after the data was retrieved ({kyiv(retrieved_at)} Kyiv); "
43	                     "run without --offline/--at to download fresh data")
44	    elif source.fresh:
45	        at = pd.Timestamp.now(tz="UTC")
46	    else:
47	        at = retrieved_at
48	        age_h = (pd.Timestamp.now(tz="UTC") - retrieved_at) / pd.Timedelta(hours=1)
49	        print(f"WARNING: no fresh data. Forecast is issued as of {kyiv(at)} Kyiv, when the data was\n"
50	              f"retrieved ({age_h:.1f} h ago). The alert status after that moment is unknown.\n")
51	    if at < config.DATA_START + pd.Timedelta(days=30):
52	        sys.exit("--at must be at least 30 days after 2023-01-01 so the model has history to learn from")
53	
54	    print(describe(forecast_at(events, at)))
55	    print(f"\n  Data: {source.note}")
56	
57	
58	if __name__ == "__main__":
59	    main()
60

# Data Visualization

A chart is **read by people and executed by you**. This skill turns "make it look
good" into a procedure with checks, so the result is right by construction rather
than by taste.

**The method here is design-system-agnostic.** Nothing in the procedure, the form
heuristic, the six checks, or the mark specs is specific to one product. A design
system supplies a small set of *parameters* (its ramps, a categorical order, a
diverging pair, a status palette, a texture, its surfaces, its filter components);
the method consumes them unchanged. A **validated default palette** is the
reference instance, fully specified in `references/palette.md`. To target your
brand, read that file's structure and substitute its values - touch nothing else.

> The single most important habit: **the color part is computable, so compute it.**
> Never eyeball whether a palette is colorblind-safe - run `scripts/validate_palette.js`.

## The procedure - do these in order

Color comes LAST. Most bad charts pick colors first.

1. **Pick the form.** What is the data's job - magnitude, identity, polarity, a
   single headline, change-over-time? The job picks the chart type, and sometimes
   the answer is *not a chart* (a stat tile or hero number). -> `references/choosing-a-form.md`
2. **Assign color by the job it does.** Categorical (identity), sequential
   (magnitude), diverging (polarity), or status (state) - each has one rule.
   Assign categorical hues in fixed order, never cycled. -> `references/color-formula.md`
3. **VALIDATE the palette - run the script, don't reason about Delta E.**
   `node scripts/validate_palette.js "<hex,hex,...>" --mode light` (relative to
   this skill's base directory - or load it as `<script type="module">` in the
   chart's own page, where it reads
   `data-palette` off `<body>` and logs a `console.table` report). It returns
   pass/fail on the lightness band, chroma floor, adjacent-pair CVD separation,
   the normal-vision floor, and contrast. Fix anything that FAILs before continuing. Re-run for
   `--mode dark` with that mode's surface.
4. **Apply mark specs & spacers.** Thin marks, 4px rounded data-ends anchored to
   the baseline, 2px lines, >=8px markers, a 2px surface gap between fills (stacked
   segments and adjacent bars alike) and a 2px surface ring on overlapping marks,
   selective direct labels. -> `references/marks-and-anatomy.md`
5. **Add the hover layer - by default.** An HTML/SVG chart *is* interactive; ship
   a crosshair+tooltip on line/area and a per-mark hover tooltip on bar/dot/cell.
   The only form that skips it is a bare stat tile with no plot. Hit targets bigger
   than the mark; filters in one row above the charts. -> `references/interaction.md`
6. **Final accessibility pass.** For >= 2 series a legend is always present and <= 4
   are also direct-labeled (a single series needs no legend box - the title names
   it), so identity is never color-alone; a table view exists; dark mode is **selected** - its own
   steps from the same ramps, validated against the dark surface, not an automatic
   flip; texture is available for the CVD/print/forced-colors case.
7. **Render it and look at it.** The validator checks color, not layout - open or
   screenshot the output and eyeball it for label collisions, geometry, and overflow
   before calling it done.

Then check the result against **`references/anti-patterns.md`** - it is the catalog
of what goes wrong. If your chart matches an entry, it's wrong.

## Non-negotiables (true in every design system)

- **Assign categorical hues in fixed order, never cycled.** A 9th series is never a
  generated hue - it folds into "Other," small multiples, or composite encoding.
- **One axis.** Never a dual-axis chart (two y-scales). Two measures of different
  scale -> two charts, small multiples, or indexed to a common base. *(This is the
  #1 chart mistake - see anti-patterns.)*
- **Color follows the entity, never its rank.** A filter that changes the series
  count must not repaint the survivors.
- **Sequential = one hue, light->dark. Diverging = two hues + a neutral gray
  midpoint.** Never a rainbow; never a hue at the diverging midpoint.
- **Run the validator before shipping any categorical palette.** CVD Delta E >= 8 is the
  target (OKLab ×100); 6-8 is a floor that is legal ONLY with secondary encoding. A
  normal-vision floor below 15 is a hard FAIL - full-color readers can't tell the
  pair apart; re-step it on the adjacent pairlist (secondary encoding does not excuse
  this one); under `--pairs all` cut series or facet instead - see check 4. A contrast WARN
  obligates visible labels or a table view - it is not dismissable.
- **Thin marks; a legend always present for >= 2 series (none for one), with
  selective direct labels (never a number on every point); recessive grid/axes.**
- **Text wears text tokens, never the series color** - values, labels, and legends
  stay in primary/secondary/muted ink; a colored mark beside them carries identity.
- **Status colors are reserved** (good/warning/serious/critical) and never reused
  for "series 4"; they ship with an icon + label, never color alone.

## Plugging in a design system

The method is invariant; only these parameters change per system. The reference
instance - every value filled in - is `references/palette.md`.

| Parameter | What the system provides |
|---|---|
| **Ramps** | the hue scales (named steps) the palette draws from |
| **Categorical theme** | the fixed hue order (a named theme); default + alternates |
| **Sequential hue** | the default single hue for magnitude |
| **Diverging pair** | two warm/cool poles + a neutral midpoint |
| **Status palette** | good / warning / serious / critical - steps distinct from categorical |
| **Texture fill** | one directional hand-drawn fill, used at 45° / 135° |
| **Surfaces** | light & dark chart-surface colors (the validator needs these) |
| **Filter controls** | date-range & dimension controls (behavioral spec in `interaction.md`) |

To onboard a new system: fill those rows, feed its ramps to the validator, and let
it snap each slot to the nearest passing step. Structure and rules stay as written.

## Reference files

| File | What it answers |
|------|-----------------|
| `references/choosing-a-form.md` | Which chart type / is it even a chart? |
| `references/color-formula.md` | The four jobs, the six checks, snap-to-passing |
| `references/marks-and-anatomy.md` | Mark specs, spacers, labels, figures, hero number |
| `references/interaction.md` | Tooltips & hover, filters & time ranges |
| `references/components.md` | The pieces a chart is made of - build each in plain HTML |
| `references/anti-patterns.md` | **What goes wrong - check every chart against this** |
| `references/palette.md` | **The reference palette instance** - every parameter, filled in; swap for your brand's |
| `scripts/validate_palette.js` | Runnable six-checks validator (run it; don't eyeball) |
